see Him here. And therefore lean fully to
thy feeling when it is gracious and ghostly,
and keep it tenderly and have great dainty,
not of thyself, but of it, that thou might see
and feel Jesus ever better and better. For
grace shall ever teach thee by itself if thou
wilt fall thereto, till thou come to the end.

Ch. 43] OF PERFECTION

But perchance thou beginnest to wonder why
I say one time that grace worketh all this,
and another time I say that love worketh, or
Jesus worketh. As unto this I say thus, that
when I say that grace worketh I mean love,
Jesus, and God; for all is one, and naught
but one. Jesus is love, Jesus is grace, Jesus
is God; and since He worketh all in us by His
grace for love as God, therefore may I use
what word of these four that me list after my
Stirring in this writing.

CHAPTER XLIII

HOW A SOUL, THROUGH OPENING OF THE
GHOSTLY EYE, RECEIVETH A GRACIOUS LOVE
ABLE TO UNDERSTAND HOLY WRIT: AND

- HOW JESUS, THAT IS HID IN HOLY WRIT,
SHOWETH HIMSELF TO HIS LOVERS

WHEN the soul of a lover feeleth Jesus in
prayer in the manner beforesaid, and thinketh
that it would never feel otherwise, neverthe-
less it befalleth that sometime grace putteth
silence to vocal praying, and stirreth the soul
to see and to feel Jesus in another manner.
And that manner is first to see Jesus in Holy
Writ; for. Jesus, that is all soothfastness, is
hid’ and covered therein, wound in a soft
sendal* under fair words, that He may not
* Sendal—.e., silken stuff.

THE SCALE [Bk. 2

be known nor felt but of a clean heart. For
why, soothfastness will not show itself to
enemies, but. to friends that love it and
desire it with a meek heart. For soothfast-
ness and meekness are full true sisters,
fastened together in love and charity; and
therefore is there no dividing of counsel
betwixt them two. Meekness presumeth on
soothfastness, and nothing of itself; and
soothfastness troweth well on meekness, and
so they accord wonder well. Then foras-
much as a soul of a lover is made meek
through inspiration of grace, by opening of
the ghostly eye, and seeth that it is naught
of itself but only hangeth on the mercy and
goodness of Jesus, and lastingly is borne up
by favour and help of Him only and truly
desiring the presence of Him, therefore seeth
it Jesus; for it seeth soothfastness of Holy
Writ wonderly showed and opened, above
study and travail and reason of man’s kindly
wit. And that may well be called the feeling
and the perceiving of Jesus, for Jesus is the
well of wisdom; and, by a little pouring of
His wisdom into a clean soul, He maketh the
soul wise enough to understand all Holy
Writ. Not all at once in special beholding;
but through that grace the soul receiveth a
new ableness, and a gracious habit to under-
stand it specially when it cometh to mind. -

This opening and this clearness of wit is.

Ch. 43] OF PERFECTION

made by the ghostly presence of Jesus. For
right as the Gospel saith of two disciples
going to the castle of Emmaus, burning in
desire and speaking of Jesus, how our Lord
Jesus appeared to them presently in likeness
of a pilgrim and taught them the prophecies
of Himself, and as the Gospel saith, opened
to them clearness of wit that they might under-
stand Holy Writ. Right so the ghostly
presence of Jesus openeth the wit of His
lover that it burneth in desire to Him, and
bringeth into his mind by ministration of
angels the words and the sentences of Holy
Writ, unsought and unadvised, one after
another, and expoundeth them readily, be
they never so hard nor so privy. The
harder that they be and the further from
man’s reasonable understanding, the more
delectable is the true showing. of them when
Jesus is master. It is expounded and
declared literally, morally, mystically \ and
heavenly, if the matter suffer it. By letter,
that is lightest and most plain, is bodily kind
comforted; by morality of Holy Writ the
soul is informed of vices and virtues wisely
kenning to depart the one from the other.
By mystihead it is illumined to see the works
of Jesus in Holy Church, readily to apply the
words of Holy Writ to Christ our head and
to Holy Church that is His body mystical.
And the fourth,.that is heavenly, belongeth

THE SCALE [Bk. 2

only to working of love, and that is when all
soothfastness in Holy Writ is applied to love;
and because that is most like heavenly
feeling, therefore I call it heavenly.

The lover of Jesus is His friend, not because
he hath deserved it but because Jesus of His
merciful goodness maketh him His friend
by true accord; and therefore as to a true
friend that pleaseth Him with love, not
serveth Him by dread as a thrall, He showeth
His privity. ‘Thus he saith Himself to His
apostles: Now I say that ye are my friends ;
for I make known to you all things that I have
heard of My Father.x To a clean soul that
hath the palate purified from filth of fleshly
love, Holy Writ. is lively food and sustenance
delectable. It savoureth wondrous sweetly
when it is well chewed by ghostly under-
standing. For why, the spirit of life is hid
therein, that quickeneth all the mights of
the soul and filleth them full of sweetness of
heavenly savour of ghostly delight. But
soothly he needeth to have white teeth and
sharp and well picked that should bite on
this ghostly bread; for fleshly lovers and
heretics may ‘not touch the inward flour of
it. Their teeth are bloody and full of filth,
and therefore they be fasting from feeling
of this bread. By teeth are understood the
inward wits of the soul, which in fleshly

* John xv 15.

Ch. 43] OF PERFECTION

lovers and in heretics be bloody and full of
sin and of worldly vanities. They would,
and cannot, come by curiosity of their kindly
wit to the soothfastness in knowing of Holy
Writ, for their wit is corrupt by original sin
and actual also, and is not yet healed through
grace. And therefore they do but gnaw upon
the bark without, speak they never so much
thereof; the inly savour within they feel not
of. They be not meek, they be not clean to
see it; they be not friends to Jesus, and there-
fore He showeth them not His counsel.

The privity of Holy Writ is closed under a
key sealed with a signet of Jesus’ finger, that
is the Holy Ghost; and therefore without His
love and His leave may no man come in,
He only hath the key of cunning in His
keeping, as Holy Writ saith; and He is key
Himself and He letteth in whom He will by
inspiration of His grace, and breaketh not
the seal. And that doth Jesus to His lovers;
not to all alike but to them that are specially
inspired to seek soothfastness in Holy Writ,
with great devotion in praying and with
much business in studying going before.
These may come to the finding when our
Lord Jesus will show it.

See now therefore how grace openeth the
ghostly eye, and cleareth the wit of the soul
wonderly above the frailty of corrupt kind;
it giveth the soul a new ableness, whether

THEVSGALE [Bk. 2

it will read Holy Writ, or hear or think it, to
understand truly and savourly the soothfast-
ness of it in the manner aforesaid, and to turn
readily all reasons and words that are bodily
said into ghostly understanding. And that
is no great marvel, for the same spirit that
first made it expoundeth it and. declareth it
in a clean soul in comfort of it ; and that is
the Holy Ghost. And this grace may be and
is as well in lewd as in lettered men, as anent
the substance of the true feeling of sooth-
fastness and the ghostly savour of it in
general; though they see not so many reasons
in special, for that needeth not. And when
the soul is thus abled and lightened through
grace, then it listeth to be alone sometimes,
out of letting or communing of all creatures,
that it might freely assay its instrument—
that I call its reason—in beholding of sooth-
fastness that is contained in Holy Writ.
And then there fall to mind words and reasons
and sentences enough to occupy the soul in,
full ordinately and full sadly.

And what comfort and ghostly delight,
savour and sweetness, a soul may feel then
in his ghostly work through divers illumina-
tions, inly perceivings, privy knowings and
sudden touchings of the Holy Ghost, by
assay the soul may wit but not otherwise.
And I think that he shall not err, if so be his
teeth, that be his inly wits, be kept white

Ch. 43] OF PERFECTION

and clean from ghostly pride and from
curiosity of kindly wit. David felt full great
delight in this manner work when he said
thus: How sweet are Thy speakings, Lord Jesus,
to my cheeks, over honey to my mouth.* That
is, Lord Jesus, Thy holy words indited in
Holy Writ, brought to my mind through
grace, are sweeter to my cheeks—that is, to
the affections of my soul—than honey is to
my mouth. Soothly this is a fair work and
an honest, without painful travail, to see
Jesus thus.

This is one manner of sight of Jesus as I
said before: not as He is, but clothed under
likeness of works and of words; by a mirror
and by a likeness, as the apostle saith.t Jesus
is endless might, wisdom and goodness,
righteousness, soothfastness, holiness and
mercy. And what this Jesus is in Himself
may no soul see nor hear. But by effect of
His working may He be seen through the
light of grace as thus: His might is seen by
making all creatures of naught, His wisdom
in ordinate disposing of them, His goodness
in saving of them, His mercy in forgiveness
of sins, His holiness in gifts of grace, His
righteousness in hard punishing of sin, His
soothfastness in true rewarding of good
works. And all this is expressed in Holy
Writ, and this seeth a soul in Holy Writ with

* Ps, cxviii 103. TECON Xiii 12.

THE SCALE [Bk. 2

all other accidents that fall thereto. And
wit thou well that such gracious knowings,
in Holy Writ or in other writing that are
made through grace, are naught else but
letters sent and made betwixt a loving soul
and Jesus loved; or else, if I shall say sooth-
lier, betwixt Jesus the true lover and the
souls loved of Him. He hath: full great
tenderness of love to all His chosen children
that are here closed in clay of this bodily life;
and therefore—though He be absent from
them, high hid above in the bosom of the
Father, fulfilled in delights of the blessed
godhead—nevertheless He thinketh upon
them and visiteth them full often through
His gracious ghostly presence, and comforteth
them by His letters of Holy Writ, and driveth
out of their hearts heaviness and weariness,
doubts and dreads, and maketh them glad
and merry in Him, truly trowing to all His
promises, and meekly abiding the fulfilling
of His will.

Saint Paul saith thus: All that ts written
to our teaching, it is written that, by comfort
of writing, we might have hope of salvation.*
And this is another work of contemplation,
to see Jesus in Scriptures after opening of
the ghostly eye. The cleaner the sight is in
beholding, the more comforted is the affec-
tion in the tasting. A full little savour felt

* Rom. xv 4.

Ch. 44] OF PERFECTION

in a clean soul of Holy Writ in this manner
beforesaid should make the soul set little
price by knowing of all the seven arts, or
all the world of all worldly cunnings. For
the end of all knowledge is salvation of a
man’s soul in everlasting life; and the end of
that other as for himself is but vanity and a
passing delight, unless they be turned
through grace to this end.

CHAPTER XLIV

OF THE PRIVY VOICE OF JESUS SOUNDING IN A
SOUL, WHEREBY IT SHALL BE KNOWN:
AND HOW ALL GRACIOUS ILLUMINATIONS
MADE IN A SOUL BE CALLED THE SPEAKINGS
_OF JESUS

Lo, these are fair new feelings in a clean soul,
and if a soul were fulfilled with such, it
might be said soothly that it were reformed
somewhat in feeling but not yet fully. For
why, Jesus showeth yet more, and leadeth
the soul inner, and beginneth to speak more
homely and more lovely to a soul; and ready
is the soul then to follow the stirring of grace.
For the prophet saith: Whither so goeth the
spirit, thither goeth the wheels following him.*
By wheels are understood true lovers of
* Ezekiti20.

THE SCALE [Bk. 2

Jesus, for they are found in virtue without
angle of frowardness, and lightly whirling
through readiness of will to the stirring of
grace. For after that grace stirreth and
teacheth, so they follow and work, as the
prophet saith. But they have first a full
secure assay and a true knowing of the voice
of grace, ere they may do so, that they be
not deceived by their own feigning or by the
midday fiend.

Our Lord Jesus saith thus of His lovers:
My sheep hear My voice, and I know them
and they know Me.* ‘The privy voice of
Jesus is full true, and it maketh a soul true.
There is no feigning in it nor |fantasy, nor
pride nor hypocrisy, but softness, meekness,
peace, love and charity; and it is full of
life, love and grace. And therefore, when it
soundeth in a soul, it is of so great might
sometimes that the soul suddenly layeth
aside all that there is, praying, speaking,
reading or thinking in the manner before-
said, and all manner bodily work, and
listeneth thereto fully, hearing and perceiving
in rest and in love the sweet sound of this
ghostly voice; as it were ravished from the
mind of all earthly things. And then some-
times Jesus showeth Himself in this peace as
an awful master, and sometimes as a reverent
father, and sometimes as a lovely spouse.

* Joħn x 14, 27.

Ch. 44] OF PERFECTION

And it keepeth a soul in a wonderful
reverence and in a lovely beholding of
Him, that the soul liketh well then and
never so well as then. For it feeleth so
great secureness and so great rest in Jesus,
and so much savour of His goodness, that it
would ever be so and never do other work.
It thinketh that it toucheth Jesus, and
through virtue of that unspeakable touching
it is made whole and stable in itself, reverently
beholding only Jesus, as if there were nothing
but Jesus one thing and he another, borne
up only by the savour and the wonderful
goodness of Him; that is, that thing that he
seeth and feeleth.

And this feeling is oftentimes without
special beholding of Holy Writ, nor but with
few words formed in the heart; not but that,
among these, there fall in sweet words
according to the feeling, either loving or
worshipping or wondering, or otherwise
sounding as the heart liketh. ‘The soul is
full much departed from love or liking of
the world through virtue of this gracious
feeling, and also from mind of the world
much in the meantime; it taketh none heed
thereof, for it hath no time thereto. But
then sometime anon with this there fall into
a soul divers illuminations through grace,
the which illuminations I call speakings of
Jesus and the sight of ghostly things. For

THE SCALE [Bk. 2

wit thou well that all the business that Jesus
maketh about a soul is to make it a true
perfect spouse to Him in the highness and
the fulness of love. And that may not be
done suddenly, therefore Jesus—that is love
and of all lovers the wisest—assayeth by `
many wises and by many wonderful means
ere it may come about; and therefore, that
it might come to an effect of true spousage,
He hath such gracious speakings in likeness
of a wooer to a chosen soul. He showeth
His privy jewels, much thing He giveth and
more He promiseth, and courteous dalliance
He showeth. Often He visiteth with much
grace and ghostly comfort as I before said. -
But how He doth this in special, all fully can
I not tell thee, for it needeth not. Never-
theless somewhat shall I say after that grace
stirreth.

The drawing of a soul fully to perfect love
is first by the showing of ghostly things to a
clean soul, when the ghostly eye is opened;
not that a soul should rest therein and make
an end there, but by that means should seek
Him and love Him only that is highest of all,
without any beholding of other thing than
Himself. But what are these ghostly things,
sayest thou, for I speak often of ghostly
things. To this I answer and say, that this
ghostly thing may be said to be all sooth-
fastness of Holy Writ. And therefore a soul

Ch. 45] OF PERFECTION

that through light of grace may see the
soothfastness of it, seeth ghostly things, as I
have before said.

CHAPTER XLV

HOW THROUGH GRACIOUS OPENING OF THE
GHOSTLY. EYE A SOUL IS MADE WISE,
MEEKLY AND SOOTHFASTLY, TO SEE THE
DIVERSITY OF DEGREES IN HOLY CHURCH
AS TRAVAILING, AND TO SEE THE NATURE OF
ANGELS. AND FIRST OF FALLEN ANGELS

NEVERTHELESS other ghostly things there be
also, the which through light of grace are
showed to the soul; and they are these. The
kind of all reasonable souls, and the gracious
working of our Lord Jesus in them; the kind
of angels blessed and reproved, and their
working; and the knowing of the blessed
Trinity, after that grace teacheth.

Holy Writ saith in the book of songs of
the spouse thus: J shall rise and I shall go
about the city, and I shall seek him that my
soul loveth.* That is, I shall rise unto
highness of thought and go about the city.
By this city is understood the universe of all
creatures bodily and ghostly, ordained and
ruled under God by laws of kind and of
grace. I go about this city when I behold

* Cant. iii 2.

THE SCALE [Bk. 2

the kind and the causes of bodily creatures,
the gifts of grace and the blisses of ghostly
creatures; and in all these I seek Him whom
my soul loveth. It is fair looking with the _
inner eye on Jesus in bodily creatures to see
His might, His wisdom and His goodness in
ordinance of their kind, but it is much fairer
looking on Jesus in ghostly creatures. First
in reasonable souls chosen and reproved, to
see the merciful calling of Him to the chosen,
how He turneth them from sin by light of
His grace; how He helpeth them, teacheth
them, chasteneth them, comforteth them;
how He righteth, He cleanseth, He feedeth;
how He maketh them burning in love and
in light by plenty of His grace. And this
doth He not to one soul only, but to all His
chosen, after measure of His grace. Also of
all reproved, how rightfully He forsaketh
them and leaveth them in their sins, and
doth them no wrong; how He rewardeth
them in this world, suffering them to have
the fulfilling of their will, and after punisheth
them endlessly.

Lo, this is a little beholding of Holy
Church, whiles it is in travailing in this life,
to see how black and how foul it seemeth in
souls that are reproved, how fair and how
lovely it is in chosen souls. And this ghostly
sight is naught else but the sight of Jesus, not
in Himself but in His merciful privy works

Ch. 45] OF PERFECTION

and in His righteous dooms, every day
showed and remembered and renewed to
reasonable souls. Also over this to see with
the ghostly eye the pains of reproved and
the bliss of chosen souls, it is full comfortable.
For soothfastness may not be seen in a clean
soul without great delight and wonderful
softness of blessed burning love.

Also the sight of angels’ kind; first of
damned and after that of the blessed. It is
a full fair contemplation of the fiend in a
clean soul, when grace bringeth the fiend to
the sight of the soul as a clumsy caitiff bound
with the might of Jesus that he may not
hurt any. Then the soul beholdeth him, not
bodily but ghostly, seeing his kind and his
malice; and turneth him upside down, and
spoileth him and rendeth him all to naught,
scorneth him and despiseth him, and setteth
naught by his malice. Thus biddeth Holy
Writ when it saith thus: Turn the wicked,
that is the fiend, upside-down, and he shall
be as naught.* Much wonder hath the soul
that the fiend hath so much malice and so
little might. There is no creature so
unmighty as he is, and therefore it is a great
cowardice that men dread him so much.
He may nothing do without leave of our Lord
Jesus, not so much as enter into a swine, as
the Gospel saith. Much less then may he

* Prov. xii 7.

THE SCALE [Bk. 2

do to annoy any man. And then if our Lord
Jesus give him leave to tarry us, it is full
worthily and mercifully done that our Lord
Jesus doth, and therefore welcome be our
Lord Jesus by Himself and by all His .
messengers. The soul dreadeth no more
then the blustering of the fiend, than the
stirring of amouse. Wondrous wrath is the
fiend if it durst say nay, but his mouth is
stopped with his own malice, his hands are
bound as a thief worthy to be deemed and
hanged in hell, and then the soul accuseth
him and rightfully deemeth him after that
he hath deserved. Wonder not at this
saying, for Saint Paul meant the same when
he said thus: Brethren wit ye not well that
we shall deem angels,* that are wicked spirits
through malice, that were made good angels
by kind, as who saith yes. This deeming is
figured before the doom in contemplative
souls; for they feel a little tasting in likeness
of all that shall be done afterward by our
Lord openly in soothfastness.

Shamed and shent} is the fiend greatly
in himself, when he fares thus with a clean
soul. He would fain flee away and he may
not, for the might of the Highest holdeth him
still, and that grieveth him more than all
the fire of hell. Wondrous meekly falleth

AOO navies:
+ Shent—7.e., disgraced.

“Ch. 46] OF PERFECTION

the soul to Jesus then, with hearty laudings
that He so mightily saveth a simple soul from
all malice of so fell an enemy by His great
mercy. .

CHAPTER XLVI

HOW BY THE SAME LIGHT OF GRACE THE
BLESSED ANGELS’ NATURE MAY BE SEEN.
AND HOW JESUS IS GOD AND MAN ABOVE
ALL CREATURES, AFTER THAT WHICH THE
SOUL MAY SEE HERE l

AND then after this, by the same light, may
the soul see ghostly the fairhead of angels,
the worthiness of them in kind, the subtlety
of them in substance, the confirming of
them in grace, and the fulness of endless
bliss; the sundrihead of orders, the dis-
tinctions of persons, how they live all in
light of endless soothfastness, and how they
burn all in love of the Holy Ghost after: the
worthiness of orders, how they see and love
and praise Jesus in blessed rest without
ceasing. There is no sight of body nor no
figure in imagination in this manner working,
but all ghostly of ghostly creatures.

Then beginneth the soul to have great
acquaintance of the blessed spirits, and a
great fellowship. They are full tender and
full busy about such a soul to help it, they
are masters to teach it, and often, through

393 P

THE SCALE [Bk. 2

their ghostly presence and touching of their
light, they drive out fantasies from the soul.
They illumine the soul graciously, they
comfort the soul by sweet words suddenly
sounded in a clean heart; and, if any dis-ease
fall ghostly, they serve the soul and minister -
to it all that it needeth. Thus Saint Paul
said of them: Wot ye not well that all holy
spirits are ministers, sent of Fesus for them that
take the heritage of health.x These are
chosen souls. As who saith yes. For wit
thou well that all this ghostly working of
words and of reasons brought to the mind,
and such fair likeness, are made by the
ministering of angels, when the light of grace .
abundantly shineth in a clean soul. It may
not be told by tongue, the feelings, the .
lightenings, the graces and comforts in
special, that clean souls perceive through
favorable fellowship of blessed angels. The
soul is well at ease with them to behold how
they do, that it would attend to naught else.

But then, with the help of the angels, the
soul seeth yet more. For knowing riseth
above all this in a clean soul; and that is to
behold the blessed kind of Jesus. First of
His blessed manhood, how it is highed
worthily above all angels’ kind: and then
after of His blessed godhead, for by knowing
of creatures is known the Creator. And then

* Heb. i 14.

Ch. 46] OF PERFECTION

beginneth the soul to perceive a little of the
privities of the Blessed Trinity. It may well
enough, for light of grace goeth before, and
therefore she shall not err as long as she
holdeth her with the light.

Then is opened soothfastly to the eye of
the soul the onehead in substance and dis-
tinction of persons in the Blessed Trinity,
as it may be seen here, and much other
soothfastness of the Blessed Trinity pertinent
to this matter; the which is openly declared
and showed by writings of holy doctors of
Holy Church. And wit ye well that the self-
same soothfastness of the Blessed Trinity,
that inspired these holy doctors through grace
to write in their books in strengthening of
our troth, a clean soul may see and know
through the self light of grace. I will not
express so much of this matter here specially
for it needeth not.

Wondrous great love feeleth the soul with
heavenly delight in feeling of this soothfast-
ness, when it is made through special grace;
for love and light go both together in a clean
soul. There is no love that riseth of knowing
and of special beholding that may touch so
near our Lord as this love may; for why, this
knowing of Jesus God and man is worthiest
and highest in itself only, if it be specially
showed by the light of grace. And therefore
is the fire of love flaming of this more burn-

THE SCALE [Bk. 2

ing, than it is of knowing of any creature
bodily or ghostly. And all these gracious
knowings felt in a soul, of the universe of
all creatures in manner beforesaid, and of our

Lord Jesus maker and keeper of all this fair -

universe, I call them fair words and sweet
speakings of our Lord Jesus to a soul which
He would make His true spouse.

He showeth privities and proffereth rich
gifts of His treasure, and arrayeth the soul
with them full honestly. She need not be
ashamed with the company of her fellows to
appear afterwards to the face of Jesus her

spouse. All this lovely dalliance of privy |

speech betwixt Jesus and a soul may -be
called an hid word, of the which Holy Writ
saith thus: Soothly to me is said an hid word,
and the veins of his whisperings mine ear hath
perceived.* The inspiration of Jesus is an
hid word, for it is privily hid from all lovers
of the world, and showed to His lovers;
through the which a clean soul perceiveth
readily the veins of His whisperings, that
are specially showings of His soothfastness.
For each and every gracious knowing of
soothfastness felt with inly savour and
ghostly delight, is a privy whisper of Jesus
to the ear of a clean soul. It behoveth him
to have much cleanness and meekness and
all other virtues, and to be half deaf to noise
* Job iv 12.

Ch. 46] OF PERFECTION `

of worldly jangling, that should wisely
perceive these sweet ghostly whisperings.
This is the voice of Jesus, of which David
saith thus: The voice of our Lord Jesus
assaying the harts, and He shall show the
thicket.* ‘That is, the inspiration of Jesus
maketh souls light as harts that start from
the earth over bushes and briars of all worldly
vanity; and He showeth to them the thicket,
that is His privities, that may not be perceived
but by sharp eyes. These beholdings,
soothfastly grounded in grace and in meek-
ness, make a soul wise and burning with
desire to the face of Jesus. These are the
ghostly things that I spake of before, and
they be called new gracious feelings. And I
do but touch them a little for wissing} of thy
soul. For the soul that is clean stirred by
grace to use of this working, may see more
in an hour of such ghostly matter, than might
be writ in a great book.

Thus tinisheth this present book which
expoundeth many notable doctrines in con-
templation: which are, meseemeth, right
expedient to those that set their felicity in
occupying themselves specially for their
soul’s health.

Infinite laud with thankings manifold

I yield to God, me succouring with His grace,
This book to finish, which that ye behold,
Scale of Perfection called in every place.
Whereof the author Walter Hilton was:

And Wynkyn de Worde this hath set in print
In William Caxton’s house, so fell the case.
God rest his soul, in joy there mote it stint.

This heavenly book, more precious than gold,
Was late direct with great humility,

For godly pleasure thereon to behold,

Unto the right noble Margaret as ye see,
The king’s mother, of excellent bounty,
Henry the seventh, that Jesus him preserve.
This mighty princess hath commanded me
To imprint this book her grace for to deserve.
